## Questions raised during implementation

| # | Task | Question | Conservative choice taken | Answer (human) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q-0 | T-002 | The POC (`poc/QbAutopost`) is not available. Will it be supplied? | Core written from the spec (ADR-0007); every item below is a guess to confirm | Owner: build from spec (2026-09-16) |
| Q-1 | T-002 | What is the `rules.json.CsvLayouts` format, and what are the real bank/card CSV columns? | Layout = `HeaderContains[]` + column names + `DateFormat` + one signed `AmountColumn` (`PositiveIsDebit`) or `DebitColumn`/`CreditColumn`, plus optional check-number/balance/last-four columns. No layout or 2+ layouts match → statement held. A date that fails the layout format is a row error (no fallback). Sample layouts imitate Chase exports | |
| Q-2 | T-002 | What does `Normalize(description)` do? | Trim, collapse whitespace, upper-case invariant; digits and punctuation kept. Fuzzy matching also drops apostrophes and turns other punctuation into spaces | |
| Q-3 | T-002 | Fingerprint field spelling (kind, direction, missing check number)? | `bank`/`card`, `debit`/`credit`, missing check number = "" | |
| Q-4 | T-002 | G1 "both orientations" — row order, balance sign, or both? | Row order only (file order or reversed). The sign is fixed by kind: bank credits raise the balance, card charges raise it. A partly filled balance column fails G1 | |
| Q-5 | T-002 | Which columns do the paste-ready sheets have, and in what date format? | Checks: Bank, Date, Number, Payee, Account, Amount, Memo. Card: Card, Date, Number, Payee, Type, Account, Amount, Memo. Deposits: Date, Received From, From Account, Memo, Amount, Deposit To. ISO dates, UTF-8 BOM, CRLF; text cells starting with `= + - @` get a leading `'` | |
| Q-6 | T-002 | What is the base `rules.json` schema, and what are the defaults? | Keys as in `Rules.cs`; `FuzzyThreshold` 0.85, `HistoryMinCount` 3. Missing `HoldingExpenseAccount` → numbered checks held; missing `DepositIncomeAccount` → deposits held. Pattern `Match` is a plain substring, not a regex | |
| Q-7 | T-002 | What happens when a check number is over 11 characters, or several aliases / equal fuzzy scores match? | Line held (`refnumber-too-long` / `unknown-payee` with a note); never truncated or guessed. Memo over 4095 characters is truncated (no money impact) | |
| Q-8 | T-001 | CLAUDE.md's NuGet list doesn't include the xUnit runtime packages (`Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk`, `xunit`, `xunit.runner.visualstudio`) | Added — xUnit tests can't run without them | |
| Q-9 | T-002 | File name with several 4-digit groups (e.g. `2026-chase-4521.csv`)? | No last-four taken from the name; fall back to the content, else hold `unknown-account` | |
| Q-10 | T-002 | One unparseable row in a CSV: hold the row or the whole statement? | Whole statement held (`unparsable-rows`), because a missing row makes the statement incomplete | |
| Q-11 | T-003 | How should `requirement.txt` state real last-four values? | Assumed as 4-digit groups on the account lines ("Bank Account=… 4521"). Please share a real example | |
| Q-12 | T-101 | Which characters may a job folder name (= job id) contain? | Only letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`; anything else → 400. The id appears in URLs and in batch ids `<jobId>#<attempt>` | |
| Q-13 | T-101 | Subfolders inside `statements\` / `invoices\`? | Not read; each is listed in `result.json.unreadable[]` with reason `subfolder-ignored`. `.jpeg`/`.tif` invoices are `unsupported-extension` (spec lists only pdf/png/jpg) | |
| Q-14 | T-102 | Where are the "known job folders" (spec §6 `GET /jobs`, startup recovery) recorded? | New setting `Paths.JobIndex` (default `jobs.json`) maps job id → folder and is rewritten atomically when a job is first saved. A job found `queued` at startup is set `failed (interrupted)`, not re-queued, because it might be a non-dry run | |
| Q-15 | T-103 | Must the company named in `requirement.txt` match `Company.Name`? | Yes: a different name (compared ignoring case and punctuation) fails G2, because the app always posts to the configured company file. The `Company` placeholder or no name → the configured name is used. An empty `Company.Name` fails every job | |
| Q-16 | T-103 | Statement-level hold codes the spec does not name | `reconcile-failed` (G1), `extractor-not-available` (xlsx/pdf until M3), `kind-mismatch` (layout says bank but the requirement lists the last-four only as a card, or the reverse) | |
| Q-17 | T-103 | Line-level: identical lines in one job (same fingerprint → same requestID), and kinds the requirement did not ask for | Every copy of an identical line is held `duplicate-line` (never post one of them); a postable line whose kind is not in the spec's kinds is held `kind-not-requested`. Precedence: skip-pattern → duplicate-line → already-posted → kind-not-requested | |
| Q-18 | T-103 | What happens when the QuickBooks call itself fails? | Any exception → job `partial`, every sent line held `quickbooks-no-response`, and the batch is recorded in the ledger with 0 posted, so `POST /jobs` refuses a re-run until someone checks QuickBooks and uses `force`. `QuickBooksUnavailableException` (thrown only before anything is sent) → `partial` "nothing posted", no ledger record. A response line with status 0 but a different echoed amount is held `amount-mismatch`, and its note names the TxnID to delete | |
| Q-19 | T-104 | API key defaults | An empty `Api:ApiKey` stops the host at startup; the shipped `change-me` is accepted only in Development. `appsettings.Development.json` uses `dev` (plan §4 curl) and local `data/` paths | |
| Q-20 | T-105 | result.json fields beyond spec §10 | Adds `company`, `error`, `counts.posted`, `totals.posted`; held items carry `file`, `lineNo`, `kind`, `note`, `candidates`. `unmatchedInvoices` stays `[]` until M4. The sample's `invoices/home-depot-88213.txt` stand-in is reported `unsupported-extension` (invoices accept pdf/png/jpg only) | |
| Q-21 | T-201 | Should a Hermes transport failure (timeout, connection refused, HTTP 5xx, malformed envelope) be retried, and must the `Authorization` header be sent when `Hermes:ApiKey` is empty? | No retry: spec §9 names only the validation retry, and the caller already falls back (T1) or holds (T2–T4). Empty key → no header (a local Hermes may run without one). Audit copies are written for every HTTP attempt, including failed ones | |
| Q-22 | T-202 | FR-2 falls back to the regex parser when T1 fails validation twice. What if Hermes is unreachable (timeout, refused, HTTP 5xx)? | The job fails (`analysis failed: Hermes Spec call failed: …`) instead of falling back, because the regex reading was not what the operator expected and the rule is to post less when unsure. Re-submit once Hermes is back. Changing this to a fallback is one `catch` in `HermesSpecReader` | |
| Q-23 | T-301 | FR-3 says a date that fails the layout format falls back to invariant `DateOnly.TryParse`; Q-1 chose "row error, no fallback". Which wins, and how are XLSX date cells handled? A workbook that cannot be opened? | Q-1 kept (session 4, owner said go ahead with it): a text date must match the layout format, otherwise the row fails and the statement is held, because an invariant (US) parse can silently swap day and month. XLSX real date cells are read as their date (`yyyy-MM-dd`), so the XLSX parser also accepts an exact ISO date; CSV does not. A plain-number "date" is a row error. A formula with no saved result is blank → row error. A workbook ClosedXML cannot open → statement held `unreadable-statement`. Following the spec instead is one `\|\|` in `StatementGridParser.TryParseDate` | |
| Q-24 | T-302 | FR-3 "text per page < 40 characters → scanned": are spaces and line breaks counted? What if OCR is enabled but a scanned page has no images, or an image OCR cannot read? Which OCR languages? | Whitespace is not counted (a page of blanks is scanned). OCR disabled → any scanned page holds the whole file, even a near-blank last page. OCR enabled: a scanned page without images keeps its own short text (nothing to OCR; T2 and G1 still check the rows); an image that is neither JPEG nor decodable by PdfPig, or any OCR error → file held `unreadable-statement`. English (`eng`) only. `Ocr:Enabled` with missing language data stops the host at startup | |
| Q-25 | T-303 | §9.2 T2: reason codes for a Hermes failure; how statement-level fields (kind, last-four, period, balances, count) combine across chunks; which last-four wins; is a statement with zero rows valid? | `hermes-failed` (validation twice or unreachable; later parts are not sent) and `extraction-conflict`. Each field must be the same in every part that reports it (null = not shown); any difference holds the statement — including a "balance forward" misread as the opening balance on a later page. File-name last-four wins (spec F5); a different one in the text → `conflicting-last4` (since T-305 the same for a CSV/XLSX account column). Zero rows is accepted here; G1 (T-304) decides with the totals. `transactionCount` is only what the statement prints; the model is told never to count | |
| Q-26 | T-304 | FR-4's T2 formula `opening + Σcredits − Σdebits = closing` fits bank statements. For cards? And what if the T2 answer has no opening or closing balance (FR-4 only says CSV/XLSX without totals pass `not-verifiable`)? Partly printed running balances? | Card: charges (debits) raise the balance, as for CSV G1. T2 without both opening and closing balance → G1 fails `not-verifiable`, statement held — model-read amounts are never posted unchecked (CLAUDE.md rule 3). Running balances printed on only some rows are checked between the printed ones (for CSV a partly filled balance column still fails, Q-4). A zero-row statement passes when opening = closing (and the printed count, if any, is 0) | |
| Q-27 | T-304 | FR-10 re-runs the analysis before posting. For PDFs that means a second T2 call, whose rows could differ from the dry run the operator reviewed (still G1-checked). Should posting reuse the reviewed `rows.json` when the PDF is unchanged? | Not decided; today T2 is called again (temperature 0) and the post uses whatever passes G1 then. Suggest before M6: store the PDF's SHA-256 in `rows.json` and reuse those rows when the hash matches, so what is posted is what was reviewed. Since T-402 the same applies to invoices (T3 is called again at post time; `invoices/<file>.json` could be reused the same way) | |
| Q-28 | T-401 | §9.3 T3: hold codes for invoices, long invoices, and how the model tells vendor from customer | `unreadable` (plan M4) for an image with OCR off, an unopenable PDF, an OCR error, no text, or text over 60 000 characters (one request per invoice, never chunked, so a total is never read from part of an invoice); `scanned-pdf-ocr-disabled` for a scanned PDF with OCR off; `hermes-failed` for a Hermes failure. The job's company name is sent as "Our company" so the model can decide the role. A held invoice never holds a line; how it is reported (`result.json.unreadable[]` vs `unmatchedInvoices[]`) is decided in T-402. Sample `invoices/home-depot-88213.txt` still reported `unsupported-extension`; T-402 replaces it with the PDF fixture | |
| Q-29 | T-402 | FR-5 matching details the spec leaves open: invoice without a date; no candidate in the preferred direction; what "payee similarity" compares before payees are resolved; one line matched by several invoices; which lines are candidates; where unreadable invoices are reported | No date → no match (`no-invoice-date`). No same-direction candidate → all candidates go to the similarity step. Similarity = `Fuzzy.MatchScore(line description, invoice party)` (the FR-6 payee is resolved later). A line matched by 2+ invoices → none of them match (`ambiguous`, note names the other files). Candidates = every line of a statement that passed G1. No candidate → `no-matching-line`. Unreadable/`hermes-failed` invoices are listed in `result.json.unmatchedInvoices[]` with their hold code as `reason` (not in `unreadable[]`, which stays the folder-level F2 list); answers Q-28's open point | |
| Q-30 | T-403 | FR-5 "supplies Payee (if the line has none)": which value is `InvoiceRef`; may the invoice party be used when it is not a QuickBooks name; which lines and roles qualify | `InvoiceRef` = invoice file name (unique in the job; the invoice number may be missing). The party is used only when it resolves to a known vendor/customer (QuickBooks lists, `VendorAccounts` keys, ledger payees, aliases) — otherwise QuickBooks would reject the line or a new name would be invented. Only lines that take a payee (FR-6 table) and only a fitting role: vendor invoice → card line / ACH debit, customer invoice → deposit. Numbered checks ("Blank if Check") and transfers never get one | |
| Q-31 | T-501 | §9.4 T4: how is the account list filtered when only some accounts have a type; is OtherIncome allowed; must `alternatives` be listed names; what if there are no accounts? | When any account has a type, untyped accounts are dropped and only Expense, Income, CostOfGoodsSold and OtherExpense are kept (OtherIncome is not named in §9.4); when none has a type, all are kept. An unlisted alternative does not reject the answer (§9.4 rejects only on `account`) but is dropped from `candidates`, so a held line never suggests an unknown name. No accounts (qb-lists.json not synced) → line held `no-accounts`, Hermes not called. A list violation is retried once like any invalid answer, then `hermes-failed` | |
| Q-32 | T-502 | FR-6/FR-7 tiers 3–4 and G3: what happens when the invoice-hinted answer is below the threshold; which invoices count as tier-3 evidence; G3 hold codes; what "prior posting to the same account" compares; is the threshold range checked? | A tier-3 answer below the threshold becomes the tier-4 answer (Hermes is not asked again without the hint) and G3 holds it. Only a matched **vendor** invoice with a non-blank `categoryHint` is evidence (tiers only run for vendor lines). Holds: `low-confidence` (score < threshold, checked first), `no-prior-posting` (no live ledger entry with the same payee and line account, compared ignoring case as in tier 2). Held model lines show no `lineAccount`; the suggestion is `candidates[0]` and in the note. T4 runs only for lines that passed the job gates. `ModelConfidenceThreshold` must be in (0, 1], else `rules.json` fails to load and the job fails | |
| Q-33 | T-503 | §10 lists one `confidence` field per `analysis.json` line. Is it the FR-6 category (rule/history/invoice/model/holding/hold) or the Hermes T4 score? | Both are written: `confidence` keeps the category (a line held by G3 reads `hold` with `tier` 4) and the score is an extra field `modelConfidence` (0…1, null when the model was not asked). `result.json` / `JobView` held items do not show the score | |
| Q-34 | T-601 | Plan T-601 says DI picks the fake gateway when `QuickBooks:Fake=true` or the host is not Windows, but a fake that answers "posted" would put invented TxnIDs in the real ledger. Also: what happens to a call still running when the busy timeout fires, and may another call start meanwhile? | `QuickBooks:Fake=true` → an in-memory simulated company (`SimulatedQuickBooks`, TxnIDs `SIM-n`), allowed only in the Development or Testing environment (startup fails otherwise). Not Windows and not faked → `UnconfiguredQbGateway`: nothing is ever sent and posting ends `partial` "nothing posted". Every QuickBooks call (worker, undo, sync-lists, health) goes one at a time through `ResilientQbGateway`. A call past `BusyTimeoutSeconds` is abandoned (`quickbooks-busy`; lines held and batch recorded as in Q-18) but keeps the lock until the COM call really ends; a caller that cannot get the lock within the busy timeout gets `quickbooks-unavailable` (nothing sent). Empty `Company:FilePath` → unavailable. `QuickBooks:RetryDelaySeconds` (default 5) exists only so tests do not wait | |
| Q-35 | T-602 | FR-15 details: which name field is stored, how `missingInRules` compares names, where sync's audit copies go (FR-17 names only job files), and what happens when QuickBooks refuses a list query? | Accounts and customers store `FullName` (sub-accounts `Utilities:Electric`, jobs `Customer:Job`), vendors `Name`; `AccountType` kept as the type. `missingInRules` = distinct account names in `BankAccounts`, `CardAccounts`, `VendorAccounts`, `KeywordAccounts`, `TransferPatterns`, `HoldingExpenseAccount`, `DepositIncomeAccount` not found **exactly** (ordinal) in QuickBooks, so a case difference is reported. Audit copies: `qb-audit/sync-lists.request.qbxml` / `.response.qbxml` next to `qb-lists.json` (overwritten each sync). Queries use `onError="stopOnError"`; status 1 (no match) = empty list; any other status → 502, and `qb-lists.json` is not changed. Unreadable `rules.json` → nothing sent, 500. The sync waits for the job worker (it may wait behind a running job) | |
| Q-36 | T-603 | FR-8 G4 details: how the query filters, how payees and RefNumbers compare, what a deposit's "payee" is, what happens when a query fails, and how the audit files are named (FR-17 says `query-<n>.qbxml`) | One message set per (kind, header account, compared ignoring case) with `TxnDateRangeFilter` + `AccountFilter`; results whose header account differs are ignored (QuickBooks' `AccountFilter` also matches line accounts). Same amount = exactly equal. "Exact" = same date and (same RefNumber other than `ACH`, ignoring case, or same payee ignoring case); a deposit's payee is its first line's `EntityRef` (`IncludeLineItems`). Several candidates → the first exact one skips the line, else one hold note lists them all. A refused or unreadable query → that group's lines held `duplicate-check-failed`; a gateway failure (unavailable, busy, COM) → nothing is added, job `partial` "nothing posted", no ledger record. Files: `output/query-<n>.qbxml` (request) and `query-<n>.response.qbxml`, old ones deleted at the start of each post. A skipped line's note (TxnID) is now shown in `result.json.skipped[].note` | |
| Q-37 | T-604 | FR-11 retries "a COMException" once after 5 s, but a COM error from `ProcessRequest` on an add may already have posted the lines. And FR-11 says a stale backup makes the job `failed`, while §6 allows only `posting → posted/partial` | Retried once: a session that could not open (nothing sent) and a COM error on a read-only (query) message set. **Not** retried: a COM error on an add or delete (lines held `quickbooks-no-response`, batch recorded, Q-18), a busy timeout, or a wait for a still-running abandoned call. Backup guard runs in `posting` just before G4 and only when there is something to post: blank `BackupFolder` → no check; missing folder, no `*.QBB` (top level only, ignoring case), or newest `LastWriteTimeUtc` older than `BackupMaxAgeHours` → job `failed` with `backup-too-old: …`, nothing sent, no ledger record (so a plain re-submit works). `posting → failed` was added to the state machine for this case only | |
| Q-38 | T-606 | FR-13 details: what counts as a successful delete, what happens to a transaction someone already deleted by hand (QuickBooks answers 3120 "cannot be found"), a batch with no posted lines (Q-18), a QuickBooks failure mid-undo, the job status, and where the audit copies go | Marked `undone` only for `statusCode 0` whose echoed `TxnID` is the one requested; anything else (including 3120 and a missing answer) is reported in `failed[]` with `code: message` and stays live, so the batch and job are not undone (a person checks QuickBooks; a later undo retries only the live entries). A batch with no live entry → 200 `deleted: 0`, QuickBooks not called, nothing changed. A gateway failure → 503, nothing marked (a delete that did happen then shows up as a 3120 failure on the next undo). Unknown batch → 404. Batch ids compare ignoring case. Job `posted/partial → undone` only when its current `batchId` is the undone batch. Audit: `output/undo-<batch id with # as ->.request.qbxml` / `.response.qbxml` in the job folder, or `qb-audit/` next to the ledger when the job is unknown | |
| Q-39 | T-607 | FR-16 `/health/quickbooks` returns `{ ok, companyFile, message }` but does not say what `message` holds, where `companyFile` comes from, or whether the check waits for a running post | `ok` = `HostQuery` status 0. `message` = product name and version (e.g. `QuickBooks Desktop Pro 2023 33.0`), or the error plus the process bitness (`process is x64`), the usual cause of a failed session. `companyFile` = the SDK's `GetCurrentCompanyFileName` in a second session; if only that fails, `ok` stays true with `companyFile: null` and the reason in `message`. The check is not queued behind jobs but waits for the gateway lock, so during a post it may take up to `BusyTimeoutSeconds` and then return 503. It does not compare the open file with `Company:FilePath` | |
| Q-40 | T-701 | FR-14 details: `rules.json` has `//` comments that a rewrite cannot keep; must the alias name / vendor also be in `qb-lists.json` (spec §6 names only the account); how short may a fragment be; what if `rules.json` is broken or missing; does teaching wait for a running job? | Comments are dropped on the first taught rule (the file is re-serialised through `JsonNode`, other keys and their order kept; sections found ignoring case, created when missing). When `qb-lists.json` exists, the account, the vendor (`/rules/account`) and the alias name (vendor or customer list by `kind`) must all be in it **exactly** (ordinal, as Q-35); a case-only difference is refused with "Did you mean …". Without lists anything non-blank is accepted. Fragment is stored normalised (Q-2) and needs ≥ 3 characters; an existing fragment with the same normalised form, or a vendor key equal ignoring case, is replaced and returned as `previous`. `kind` other than `vendor`/`customer` → 400. Broken or missing `rules.json` → 500, file untouched (never recreated). Writes use a process-wide lock, not the job worker, so they never wait for a job; a running job keeps the rules it loaded at its start | |
| Q-41 | T-702 | §14 logging details: file name and retention, which levels are configurable, what "no secrets" covers, and what the correlation id is on lines that are not about a job | File `Paths:Logs/qbautopost-yyyyMMdd.log`, a new file each day and at 1 GB, 31 files kept, shared so two hosts can write. Sinks (console + file) are fixed in code so a sink added in configuration cannot bypass scrubbing; only `Serilog:MinimumLevel:Default` / `Override:<category>` are read (defaults: Information, `Microsoft` Warning, `Microsoft.Hosting.Lifetime` Information, `System.Net.Http.HttpClient` Warning). Every event is scrubbed before any sink: the values of `Api:ApiKey`, `Hermes:ApiKey` and any configuration key ending in `ApiKey`/`Password` (or named Authorization, Token, Secret, API_SERVER_KEY) are masked `***` in the message, properties and exception text (values under 4 characters are masked only by label, e.g. the Development key `dev`); the value after `X-Api-Key:`, `Authorization: Bearer`, `apiKey=` etc. is masked; properties with such names are masked. `jobId` = the worker scope (job id, or the label `qb-sync-lists` / the batch's job id for exclusive work), else the `{JobId}` named in the message, else `-` | |
| Q-42 | T-801 | §4 says Hermes runs in Docker with port 8642 on loopback and `API_SERVER_KEY` required, but never names the image, the port the API listens on inside the container, the container's variable names for enabling the API server, where it keeps its data, or which provider key variable it needs (Q5) | `HERMES_IMAGE`, `HERMES_CONTAINER_PORT`, `HERMES_DATA_PATH` and `API_SERVER_KEY` are **required** in `deploy/hermes/.env` with no defaults, so compose refuses to start rather than pull or run a guessed image. The container gets `API_SERVER_ENABLED=true`, `API_SERVER_HOST=0.0.0.0` (container side only), `API_SERVER_PORT`, `API_SERVER_KEY`; the provider key goes in an optional, git-ignored `provider.env` under whatever name the provider needs. Host side is fixed at `127.0.0.1:8642`. Memory cap 4g by default. Needs the owner to name the image and confirm the variable names | |
| Q-43 | T-802 | Plan T-802: "WSL/Docker up → compose up → wait for `/health/hermes` → start API". But `/health/hermes` is an endpoint of the API itself, so it cannot answer before the API starts. What should the script wait for, and what if Hermes never comes up? Should it run elevated? | The script waits for Hermes' own port (`127.0.0.1:8642` accepting TCP, up to 300 s), then starts the API, then waits for `/health/hermes` = 200 (up to 180 s). If Hermes stays down the API is **still started** (undo and `/health/quickbooks` need no Hermes; a new job fails at T1 and posts nothing) and the script exits 2 with a warning; Docker/compose/API start failures exit 1 and the API is not started when Docker or compose failed. Only one API process (by exe name) is ever started. The task runs with limited rights (no elevation), interactive logon, 60 s delay; registering it needs an elevated shell. Docker Engine in WSL is started as root inside the distribution when `docker info` fails | |
| Q-44 | T-804 | Plan T-804: "go-live one company, then the rest". The app configures one `Company` (name, file, rules) and QuickBooks Desktop has one company file open per session. How are several companies run: one installation per company (own folder, port, ledger, rules, task), one at a time with QuickBooks switching files, or a multi-company app? | Nothing added: one installation = one company. `start-all.ps1` starts at most one API process (checked by exe name), so a second installation on the same server would be seen as "already running" and not started. Switch companies one at a time until the owner decides; the T-804 checklist goes live for the first company only | |
| Q-45 | T-806 | The owner wants a POC without Hermes. The spec makes Hermes part of every job (T1 reads the requirement; FR-2 falls back to regex only after two invalid answers). What should "no AI" do? | New `Hermes:Enabled` (default `true`, so production is unchanged). `false`: the requirement is read by `RegexSpecReader`; every other Hermes call fails as "unavailable", so a PDF statement is held, an invoice is unmatched and a line no rule resolves is held `hermes-failed` — nothing is guessed. `/health/hermes` answers 503 "turned off". Ask the owner whether this mode should stay after the POC | |
| Q-46 | T-913 | May we add `Scalar.AspNetCore` for the reference UI? The owner ruled out Swagger/Swashbuckle entirely (2026-09-22) and `CLAUDE.md` forbids new packages without approval. The OpenAPI document itself needs no package | The document (`wwwroot/openapi.json`, hand-authored, drift-tested against `EndpointDataSource`) ships either way; the UI waits for the answer. **No Swagger fallback** — if Scalar cannot be used, there is no UI. The version chosen must target `net8.0` and ship its assets locally (the server may have no outbound internet) | **ANSWERED 2026-09-23: yes, add `Scalar.AspNetCore`.** The owner approved the package explicitly when setting up the unattended run. It is the 7th package; `CLAUDE.md`s list must be updated with it. Served from our own host, so no CDN call. |
| Q-47 | T-911 | D-4 says remote callers. From where exactly — the same LAN, a VPN, or the public internet? It decides whether a CIDR allow-list is enough or a reverse proxy and managed certificates are needed | TLS required off-loopback (startup refuses without it unless `AllowInsecureRemote`), per-client CIDR allow-list, rate limits, no CORS origins by default | |
| Q-48 | T-910 | Who issues and revokes caller keys, and where do they live between issue and use? `clients.json` on the server is the simplest thing; it is not a secret manager | `clients.json` beside `rules.json`, git-ignored, **hash + salt only**; keys generated by `scripts/new-api-client.ps1`, printed once, never logged or written to `output/`; rotation via `previousKeyHash` + expiry | |
| Q-49 | T-906 | On the direct path there is no statement, so G1 cannot run. Is `controlTotal` an acceptable substitute, and should a mismatch refuse the whole batch or post the rows that are internally consistent? | Refuse the whole batch, post nothing, `400 control-total-mismatch`. The API never adjusts an amount to make a total agree (`CLAUDE.md` rule 3) | |
| Q-50 | T-904 | May a caller name the company file per request? That is multi-company by the back door (cf. Q-44) | No: `QuickBooks:AllowCompanyFileOverride=false`, a supplied `companyFile` is `400` and **no** gateway call is made. When enabled it must be absolute, `.QBW`, and inside `AllowedCompanyFolders[]` | |
| Q-51 | T-906 | May a direct post use Hermes to choose accounts (`allowModelAccounts`), or must every row carry `lineAccount` or match a rule? | Default `false`: an unresolved row is **held**, never guessed. `true` needs `Hermes:Enabled` **and** the `qb:post:ai` scope | |
| Q-52 | T-912 | How long must batch evidence (`request.json`, qbXML, audit lines) be kept? | 400 days for `audit-yyyyMMdd.jsonl` (money evidence), 30 days for idempotency records, 31 days for the operational log as today. All three configurable | |
| Q-53 | T-914 | When may `Api:LegacyRoutes` default to false and the flat routes be deleted? | Stays `true` until the owner confirms no caller uses the flat paths; each legacy hit is logged at Warning once per route per hour. T-914 removes the POC package's own dependency on them first | |
| Q-57 | T-905 | FR-A-5 lists a `companyName` check — compare the name QuickBooks reports with `Company:Name`. Getting that name needs a `CompanyQueryRq`, which the app has never sent: no builder, no parser, no golden file, and no way here to verify the response element names (`CompanyRet/CompanyName`?) against a real SDK. Should the check be added, and is a company **name** comparison worth it once the company **file** is already compared exactly? | Not implemented rather than guessed (rule 4). The six other checks ship, and `openInQuickBooks` already answers the dangerous version of the question — it compares the actual open file path, which is stricter than a display name. Adding `CompanyQuery` means a new qbXML request plus a golden file, and the element names must be confirmed on the server first | |
| Q-56 | T-904 | FR-A-4's example lists `openConnection`, `beginSession`, `companyQuery` and `endSession` as separately timed steps, but one `IQbGateway` call is one whole SDK session (FR-11), so those phases cannot be timed from outside without widening the gateway interface — and `QbSession` would have to report each step back per call, which is COM code only the server can verify. Is a per-phase breakdown worth that, or is "slow `hostQuery`" enough? | The three measurable steps are reported (`waitForGateway`, `hostQuery`, `companyFile`); no number is invented. A slow phase still surfaces, because the whole `hostQuery` is marked slow and carries the dialog hint — which is the action the operator needs either way. The spec's example was amended to match | |
| Q-55 | T-903 | FR-A-3 lists "supported qbXML versions readable" among the SDK checks, but the SDK exposes that list through `QBXMLVersionsForSession(ticket)`, and a ticket only exists after `BeginSession` — which the same requirement forbids, since opening a company file is exactly what this probe must avoid. Which wins? | Neither is guessed: the probe reports the **configured** `QuickBooks:QbXmlVersion` and no `supported` list. Nothing claims the configured version is accepted, so an operator is not told a version works when it may not. If the owner wants the list, the probe needs its own short session and stops being safe to call while QuickBooks is busy | |
| Q-54 | T-907 | Does the folder-based job model stay for good, or is the direct API meant to replace it once callers migrate? It decides whether `/jobs/validate` and the folder documentation are worth maintaining | Both maintained, nothing removed. One engine, two entrances; a golden test keeps their qbXML identical | |
| Q-58 | T-907 | FR-6 leaves the payee blank on a numbered check ("Blank if Check" in the requirement), but FR-A-8 gives a direct caller a `payee` field for every kind. When a caller sends a check number **and** a payee, which wins? | The caller's payee is used. Dropping a name they explicitly sent would silently discard an instruction, which is worse than a full QuickBooks record; the folder path is unchanged. This is the one place the two entrances decide differently, so it is worth confirming | |
| Q-59 | T-908 | FR-A-8 says a dry run leaves "a persisted batch in `ready` state that `POST /api/v1/batches/{id}/post` can later post", but that route is in neither the §3 route table nor the M9 task list. Should it exist, or does a caller re-send the batch with `dryRun:false`? | Not built. Re-sending is one code path instead of two, and it re-runs every gate against the rules and ledger **as they are at posting time** rather than posting a plan made earlier — which is the safer of the two, since a `ready` batch could otherwise post an account a rule has since changed | |
| Q-60 | T-909 | FR-A-12 says what a replay and a reuse do, but not what becomes of a key whose request **failed**. Should a key be held for ever once used, or released when nothing was accepted? | Released on any non-2xx. Holding it would lock a caller out of a key they chose because of a typo, and — worse — a `503` from an unreachable QuickBooks (where nothing posted) would permanently block the retry that response exists to invite. Safe because idempotency is the convenience layer: G4 and the ledger catch a genuine repeat with or without a key | |
| Q-61 | T-910 | `docs/spec.md` §6 leaves **all** of `GET /health/*` open; `docs/spec-api-v1.md` §2.2 opens only `/health` and `/health/ready`. Which applies, and has the owner's monitoring been told? | The narrower rule, on both surfaces. `/health/quickbooks` opens a QuickBooks session and `/health/sdk` probes COM, so with remote callers (D-4) leaving them open lets a stranger make the server hammer QuickBooks. One rule for both surfaces, because a rule that depends on which path you used is one nobody remembers. **An operator polling those routes without a key will start getting 401 after this upgrade** - they should be told first | |
| Q-62 | T-911 | One Api test run reported `Failed: 1, Passed: 404` immediately after the `appsettings.json` edit; the name scrolled away and it did not recur in 11 further full runs of the same code. Which test was it, and is it a real race or a pre-existing polling flake? | None taken. Recorded rather than written off: T-911 adds no shared mutable state to the default test configuration (the limiter and brake are inert unless a test enables them, and each `WithSettings` host has its own container), so the suspicion falls on an existing timing test - but that is a hypothesis. **The next agent should re-run `dotnet test` a few times and capture the failing name if it reappears** | **Answered by T-912 (2026-09-23)**: not a race in the application. Two names captured - `SyncListsApiTests.Should_Return503AndKeepFile_When_QuickBooksIsUnavailable` and `RouteVersioningTests.Should_NotRequireApiKey_When_RouteIsLiveness("/api/v1/health/ready")` - both with the same stack: `System.IO.IOException ... 'qbautopost-20260923.log' ... used by another process` thrown from `TempDir.Dispose`, **after** the test body had passed. `WebApplicationFactory.Dispose` returns before Serilog's shared file sink releases the handle. Measured: 0 failures in 3 runs without T-912, 1 in 3 with it (42 more tests, 13 more hosts), 0 in 6 after `TempDir.Dispose` was made to retry. No assertion changed |
| Q-63 | T-911 | FR-A-17 requires every caller path to sit inside `Paths:AllowedJobRoots`, and §12 ships that list **empty**. Enforcing an empty list literally refuses every job, so no installation would start; ignoring it is the permissive reading rule 4 forbids once callers are remote. Which did the owner mean? | Empty means **unconfigured**: unrestricted while the bind is loopback, and **startup refuses a non-loopback bind while it is empty** - the same shape as the TLS refusal in FR-A-14. Today's loopback deployment is unaffected; exposing the port forces the owner to say where jobs live. Same rule for `QuickBooks:AllowedCompanyFolders`, which is additionally gated by `AllowCompanyFileOverride` (false by default). Marked `// SPEC-GAP T-911` | |
| Q-64 | T-912 | FR-A-16 audits every *authenticated* mutating request. Does a request whose key was **not** recognised (401), or one refused by the brute-force brake (429 before the key is even compared), belong in the audit file too? | **No line.** The word in the FR is "authenticated", and the operational log already records every refusal with its address and the reason. Auditing unauthenticated traffic would let anyone holding a wrong key grow the money-evidence file at will, and would bury the postings it exists to record. A **403** *is* audited, because the caller is identified - that is the case the middleware was placed in front of the key check for. Marked `// SPEC-GAP T-912` | |
| Q-65 | T-912 | FR-A-16 lists `outcome` and `totalAmount` as fields but never says what values they take. Which vocabulary, and is `totalAmount` the money submitted or the money that posted? | `outcome` is one of `ok` (2xx), `accepted` (202), `replayed` (an `Idempotency-Key` replay), `refused` (4xx) or `failed` (5xx or an unhandled exception) - what became of the request, not what the caller asked for. `totalAmount` is the batch's `totals.posted`, **the money that actually moved**, so a dry run records `0`; the alternative would put a real number in the money column of a batch that posted nothing. Both are one-way doors for a reader of old files, so they are recorded rather than assumed | |
| Q-66 | T-913 | **ANSWERED by the owner 2026-09-23: use the spec's literal rule - no key in Development, `health:read` everywhere else. Implemented by T-918.** (original question follows) FR-A-18 says the reference and the document are served "only when `Api:Reference:Enabled=true` … **and, in Production**, require the `health:read` scope". Does that mean Development serves them to anyone who can reach the port? | **`health:read` in every environment.** `ApiRoutes.ScopeFor` is a pure function of method and path with no environment in it, and rule 4 asks for the reading that reveals less — this page lists every route, every scope and every request shape. The cost, which the owner should know: the key travels in a **header**, so the page cannot be opened in a plain browser at all (that is already true of the Production rule the spec does state); it is read with a client that can set `X-Api-Key`. Relaxing it for Development is a one-line change in `ScopeFor` plus an `IHostEnvironment` | |
| Q-67 | T-913 | api-v1 §2.2 says `Authorization: Bearer <key>` is the **preferred** form and `X-Api-Key` the alternative, but `ApiKeyMiddleware` reads `X-Api-Key` and nothing else — every bearer request is a 401. Should the middleware learn `Authorization: Bearer`, or should §2.2 be corrected to match the code? | **Nothing changed in the middleware**: accepting a second authentication header is a change to the security surface and belongs with T-910's row, not with a documentation task. `openapi.json` declares **both** schemes and *offers* `apiKeyAuth`, whose header name a test pins to `ApiSettings.KeyHeader`; the `bearerAuth` description says plainly that this build does not accept it. Found by T-913's RED: 14 of 23 new tests failed with 401 because they had believed the spec | |
| Q-68 | T-914 | FR-A-7 gives `POST /api/v1/jobs/validate` only `200`/`422`, but `POST /api/v1/jobs` answers **400** for exactly the same folder errors (F1: missing `requirement.txt`, empty `statements\`). Two routes, one verdict, two status codes - which is right? | The validate answers **422 with the body**, because FR-A-7 names only 200/422 and FR-A-6 already settled that a validator returning a body only on success is useless. A *malformed* request - no `folder` field at all - is still 400, as is a folder outside `Paths:AllowedJobRoots`. `POST /jobs` was not changed: nothing about creating a job is made safer by moving its status code unattended. Marked `// SPEC-GAP T-914` | |
| Q-69 | T-914 | api-v1 §3 marks `POST /api/v1/quickbooks/lists/sync` as a **rename** of `POST /qb/sync-lists`, but §13's task list gave T-914 only the two new routes. Is the old spelling to be deleted, and when? | **Both are mapped.** The v1 spelling was added, the old one kept and marked `deprecated` in the OpenAPI document; nothing was deleted, because removing a working path a caller may still use is not a decision to take with nobody watching. It should go with the rest of the legacy surface when Q-53 is answered - and unlike the flat paths, `/api/v1/qb/sync-lists` is *versioned*, so it needs its own answer | |
| Q-70 | T-914 | `GET /api/v1/quickbooks/lists` returns every cached account, vendor and customer in one response. A company with tens of thousands of names would return all of them. Should it page or filter? | **No paging.** It is a local read of a file this app wrote, so nothing is unbounded that was not already on disk, and the caller's whole purpose is to pick from the list. If the owner's real chart of accounts makes the response unwieldy, the answer is a `?q=` filter rather than paging - but no test sends a large one and nobody has measured it | |
| Q-71 | T-915 | `docs/spec-api-v1.md` §6.1 says `allowModelAccounts: true` "permits tier 3-4 (Hermes) account choice"; T-915 made the code **refuse** it with 400, because nothing ever implemented it and Q-51 is unanswered. Which is right - implement the spec, or amend it? | The code refuses and says so; the spec was **left unchanged on purpose**, because "spec wins over code" means an agent must not quietly rewrite the contract to match what it built. Whichever way the owner answers, one of the two files changes. Related: Q-51 (may a model choose accounts at all) and the `qb:post:ai` scope, which is granted by `new-api-client.ps1` and confers nothing | |
| Q-72 | T-917 | FR-A-5's example request carries `requireBackup: true` and the spec never says what the flag does; it was read by nothing until T-917. What did the owner mean by it? | Taken as the only reading that makes the flag mean anything: it raises `backupFreshness` from **warning** to **error**, so a stale backup - or a server with no `QuickBooks:BackupFolder`, which cannot confirm one - makes `ok` false and the response 422. The default without the flag is unchanged. If the owner meant something narrower (say "check the backup at all"), this is wrong in the direction of refusing more. Marked `// SPEC-GAP T-917` |
| Q-73 | T-920 | A **missing** rules file now answers `503` problem details. A rules file that exists but is **corrupt or empty** still throws into a bare `500`. Should it answer `503` too - and if so, how should the handler tell a malformed `rules.json` from a caller's own malformed JSON, given both surface as `JsonException`? | Open. Not guessed at: widening the handler to `JsonException`/`InvalidDataException` would catch exceptions that can also come from a request body, and answering `503` to a caller who sent bad JSON would be wrong in the opposite direction. Wants an owner decision or a narrower exception type from the parse path |
| Q-74 | T-923 | **(spec Q-A)** Is all-or-nothing right for a 134-row statement, where one unresolved payee blocks 133 good rows? The alternative is FR-A-8's per-row holds, which already exist | Implemented as the spec states it (§5.1): any held row refuses the whole request and nothing is sent. It is the literal reading of "return required validation results if any, then post", and it posts less (rule 4). `/transactions/validate` finds every problem offline first, and takes the same body | |
| Q-75 | T-924 | **(spec Q-B)** What client timeout should be documented for a full-size batch? | None documented — it cannot be invented. The fake gateway returns instantly, so the suite can prove nothing about it. `docs/testing/T-921-T-924.tdd.md` §9 asks for the measurement on the server | |
| Q-76 | T-924 | **(spec Q-C)** Should `Idempotency-Key` be **mandatory** on this route (400 without it)? §8 makes it the only thing that returns the *original* `txnId`s after a lost response | Left optional, as §3 and api-v1 §6.5 have it. Mandating a header would break callers that were told it was optional; the OpenAPI description and the spec both say "strongly recommended" instead | |
| Q-77 | T-921 | **(spec Q-D)** On 409, is refusing right, or should a *short* bounded wait (say 2 s) be tried first? | Refuse immediately. A bounded wait is a queue with a small number on it, and "not queued" is what was asked for. `Retry-After: 5` puts the decision back with the caller | |
| Q-78 | T-923 | **New.** §6.1 says `status` is always `posted` on a 200, but §5.2 keeps G4's live half and G5, which run *after* the SDK is touched. If QuickBooks rejects one row of twelve, the other eleven have moved and none of §5's five endings describes it. What should that answer be? | 200 carrying the **true** status (`partial`, or `failed` if nothing landed) with the held rows listed. An error status would tell a caller nothing posted when some did — the T-919 defect in reverse. Marked `SPEC-GAP T-923` in `SyncPostResult.Status` | |
| Q-79 | T-925 | **New.** A request body that fails to bind — a bad enum value, a malformed date, a string where a number belongs — escapes `RequestDelegateFactory` as an unhandled `BadHttpRequestException`. The caller gets a bare `400` with no problem details and the server logs a stack trace at `ERR`, although api-v1 §2 says every error is RFC 7807. Should model binding be wrapped so it answers a problem document naming the offending path? | Yes, and the path (`$.transactions[3].kind`) is the whole value of the answer — it is what made T-925 diagnosable at all. Same class as the corrupt-rules `500` in Q-73; both want one `IExceptionHandler` rather than two. Not fixed in T-925, which was a documentation defect | |
| Q-80 | T-1001 | Approve the five packages in spec §13? Pins (newest 8.0.x / 4.x on nuget.org, 2026-10-27): `Npgsql.EntityFrameworkCore.PostgreSQL 8.0.11`, `Microsoft.EntityFrameworkCore.Design 8.0.33` (`PrivateAssets=all`), `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Identity.EntityFrameworkCore 8.0.33`, `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authentication.JwtBearer 8.0.33`, `Testcontainers.PostgreSql 4.16.0` | Nothing is added until approved. T-1002 stops if refused | **yes** (owner, 2026-10-27): all five approved |
| Q-81 | T-1001 | Should the n8n client keep `qb:post` for a transition period, so the old workflow still works? | Withdraw it at go-live (deploy checklist). The owner can keep it by not running that step | |
| Q-82 | T-1001 | Will the dashboard be served from the same site as the API? | Assume yes (same-site refresh cookie). If not, cross-site cookies plus credentialed CORS | |
| Q-83 | T-1001 | Should `/health/ready` fail as a whole when the database is down? | No. The database is reported per feature (spec §10.7) | |
| Q-84 | T-1001 | Retention: are saved requests and versions kept forever? | Kept forever. No deletion route, no job | |
| Q-85 | T-1002 | A `Completed` engine ending with a null or non-terminal `JobStatus`. What should the attempt record? | Unknown / request locked as `unknown` / 200, so an admin resolves it (posts less). Marked `SPEC-GAP T-1002` in `AttemptOutcomes.FromCompleted` | |
| Q-86 | T-1005 | §5.2 has no column for the row fingerprints that duplicate detection (§9) compares. Recompute from the payload or persist? | Recompute in the fake (SPEC-GAP T-1005) | Answered by controller ruling R6 (2026-10-28): persisted per version; T-1006 adds column row_fingerprints text[] |
| Q-87 | T-1009 | A user row with no Identity role, or with more than one: which scopes? | None (posts less): `UserGate` gives an empty scope set, so every route but `/auth/me`, `/auth/logout` and `/auth/change-password` answers 403. Marked `SPEC-GAP T-1009` in `UserGate.LoadAsync`. T-1011 should keep exactly one role per user | |
| Q-88 | T-1010 | §11.2 names no answer for `POST /auth/change-password` with a wrong **current** password. Which status? | `400 current-password-incorrect`, and the miss counts toward Identity lockout (a stolen access token must not become a password oracle). Not 401, which the dashboard would read as an expired session. Marked `SPEC-GAP T-1010` in `AuthEndpoints.ChangePasswordAsync` | |
| Q-89 | T-1011 | §5.2 lists no `user_audit` kind for an admin clearing a lockout (`POST /users/{id}/unlock`). Record it, and as what? | Recorded as kind `unlocked` (the column is free text, no constraint), actor = the admin. Marked `SPEC-GAP T-1011` in `UserAuditWriter.Unlocked`. A display-name change has no kind and is not audited | |
| Q-90 | T-1014 | Spec §7.2 says post by id adds the request's new `status`, but `status` is already the problem status number (RFC 7807) and the 200 body's post status. Which name? | `savedRequestStatus`, so the validate-and-post body is unchanged. Marked `SPEC-GAP T-1014` in `SavedRequestPoster.SavedRequestStatusField`; renaming it is one constant | |
| Q-91 | T-1014 | §7.2 locks a `failed` post as `unknown` only for `quickbooks-no-response` rows. A write that outlives the busy timeout is held as `quickbooks-busy`, which DirectPostRunner records as "may or may not have been applied". Pending or unknown? | `unknown` (posts less: returning it to pending made a double post one click away). Marked `SPEC-GAP T-1014` in `AttemptOutcomes.FromCompleted` | |
| Q-92 | T-1016 | Spec 5.4 says the app role has no `DELETE` on the append-only tables, and the brief says none anywhere. `UserEndpoints.ReplaceRoleAsync` removes the old `user_roles` row when an admin changes a user's role. Allow it? | Yes, `GRANT DELETE ON user_roles` only (no history is lost: `user_audit` records the change). Without it 4 user-management tests fail as `qbautopost_app`. Alternative if refused: change the code to UPDATE the row's `role_id` (a key column) or keep one role row per user by upsert | |
| Q-93 | M10 review | Spec §7.2 returned a `failed` post to `pending` unless a row was held for `quickbooks-no-response` (T-1014 added `quickbooks-busy`). But G5 also holds rows as `amount-mismatch` (a TxnID exists with another amount) and `quickbooks-rejected` with "status 0 but no TxnID", and G4's live check matches on amount, so a re-post double-posts. Which rule? | Controller ruling R31: an allow-list. Pending only when every held row is a pre-send gate reason or `quickbooks-rejected` with a positive status code and no TxnID (`NothingSentReasons`, `PostVerifier.IsCleanRejection`); anything else, a new reason, or no held row is `unknown`. G5 now names a TxnID returned beside a non-zero status in the note so it cannot read as a clean rejection. Spec §7.2 table amended | Ruling R31 (2026-10-28) |
| Q-94 | T-1101 | A repeated `pushId` with a **different** body: `200` existing (R-5.2), or `409`? | `409 push-id-reused` | |
| Q-95 | T-1101 | Should an edit clear the malformed-`pushId` flag? | No, it stays on. Posting is not blocked | |
| Q-96 | T-1101 | Raise `QuickBooks:BusyTimeoutSeconds` (60) after measuring a 500-row add? | Unchanged until measured | |
| Q-97 | T-1101 | Accept the audit additions (actions `flagged-duplicate`, `request-edited`, `user-session-revoked`, `user-other`; actor kinds `system` and `anonymous`)? | Added | |
| Q-98 | T-1101 | Before v1 is deprecated, should the v1 post route be retired first, because it ignores `post: false` (§13.3)? | Recorded as the first deprecation step. Nothing changes in this work | |
| Q-99 | T-1106 | Spec 8.3 is silent on: (a) the `detail` of the ten known `user_audit` kinds; (b) a `replace` of a whole row `$.transactions[i]` (only add and remove are listed); (c) the restart recovery's `post-finished` event, which has `outcome` and `error` but no `attemptId`/`status`/`postId`. | (a) the stored detail with the subject added as `userId` (the actor is often an admin, and nothing else names the user); (b) `request-edited`, `field` = `transactions[i]` ("any other path"); (c) `post-failed` with exactly the listed keys, absent ones null, so `error` is not carried. Marked `SPEC-GAP T-1106` in `AuditEventMapper`; the view does the same | |
| Q-100 | T-1109 | Spec 2 says rate limiting and logging apply to `/api/v2` exactly as to `/api/v1`, but the plan keeps `ApiRoutes.IsHealth` unchanged, so `GET /api/v2/health/database` is rate-limited per caller in the default bucket (not per address in the health bucket) and its request line is logged at Information (not Debug). Extend `IsHealth` to `/api/v2/health/database`? | Unchanged, as the plan says (the stricter of the two: a key is needed either way, and the default bucket is per caller). | Answered 2026-10-28: spec 2 is explicit; `IsHealth` extended to `/api/v2/health` — ruling R8 |
| Q-101 | T-1110 | Dashboard v2 spec 5.2 (and the plan) say a `pushId` replay answers with `Idempotent-Replayed: true`, but every replay since T-909, the v1 save and the v2 OpenAPI entry use `Idempotency-Replayed`. Which name does v2 send? | Superseded by ruling R9 | Answered 2026-10-28: ruling R9: Idempotency-Replayed only, spec typo corrected |
| Q-102 | T-1115 | Dashboard v2 spec 11 (d) compares the caller's address with the successor's `created_ip`; it is silent when either is unknown (no remote address). Is an unknown address equal to an unknown address? | No: an unknown address gets no grace, so the reuse revokes the family (the behaviour that grants less). Marked `SPEC-GAP T-1115` in `RefreshTokenService.GraceAsync` | |
| Q-103 | T-1115 | Single-UPDATE revocations (`RevokeAllAsync`/`RevokeOthersAsync` on disable, role change, password reset) can miss an in-flight v2 grace successor; disable is mitigated by the `IsDisabled` re-check on refresh. Use the until-quiet revoke there too? | Unchanged until answered (v1 shares the window) | |
| Q-104 | T-1114 | Audit action `post-failed` also covers outcome `partial` (rows did land); rename or split later? | Unchanged; `detail.outcome` says `partial` | |
| Q-105 | T-1201 | Should scanned PDFs (no text layer) be sent to the model as a file input? | No; `422 no-text` (spec §12) | |
| Q-106 | T-1201 | Should n8n switch to `POST /statement-extractions` later, so there is one prompt copy? | Out of scope; two copies, told apart by `promptVersion` (spec §12) | |
| Q-107 | T-1201 | Keep `to` exclusive (differs from R-10.2)? | Exclusive (spec §12) | |
| Q-108 | T-1201 | Does OpenRouter's strict JSON-schema mode work for the configured model with optional properties? | Try strict; fall back to non-strict with validation in code (spec §6.3) | |
| Q-109 | T-1201 | Rename the `Hermes*` code types to neutral names and delete `deploy/hermes/`? | Later clean-up; names kept in M12 (spec §14.1) | |
| Q-110 | T-1201 | Which OpenRouter model for job calls (T1–T4)? | `~google/gemini-pro-latest`, same as uploads (spec §12) | |
| Q-111 | T-1210 | An upload save whose `Idempotency-Key` is held by a request with a different body, for an extraction not yet saved: spec 7.2 item 8 covers only an already-saved extraction (409). | Store answers `KeyReused` (new `UploadSaveKind` value, the mirror of `CreateKind.KeyReused`) and writes nothing; the route (T-1213) answers it as any v2 key reuse | |
| Q-112 | T-1212 | IANA zone ids (`Asia/Kolkata`) resolve on Windows only through ICU, which `InvariantGlobalization=true` (Directory.Build.props) turns off. ICU ships with Windows 10 1903+ and Server 2019+; Linux keeps invariant mode and resolves IANA ids through tzdata. | Ruling R4: Windows builds of Api, Api.Tests and Data.Tests (and Core.Tests already) set `InvariantGlobalization=false`; Program pins the default thread cultures to invariant so formatting is unchanged; startup logs one warning when `Asia/Kolkata` does not resolve | |
| Q-113 | T-1212 | Spec 8 and 14.1 give `GET /api/v2/health` new entries, but the route did not exist under `/api/v2`, and `V2ScopeMatrixTests` pinned it closed (admin) while `V2RoutingTests` pins anonymous `401`. | Route added (v1 body + `model` + `extraction`), scope `requests:read` or `health:read` like `/health/database` (anonymous stays 401, POST stays admin). `V2ScopeMatrixTests` edited the R12 way: `/statement-extractions` and `/health` join its exception list, and its closed-route case `GET /api/v2/health` became `POST /api/v2/health`. Needs a controller ruling | |
| Q-114 | T-1212 | A model reply holding U+0000 cannot be stored as `jsonb` (R33); the spec does not say what the upload answers. | `502 extraction-failed`, nothing stored (`// SPEC-GAP T-1212b`) | |

## Decisions

| Date | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|
| 2026-09-23 | **M9 finishes as an unattended relay**: one agent per task, each updating `handoff.md` and committing before handing over to a fresh agent. The owner is away and will not answer anything mid-run | Context does not survive between agents, so `handoff.md` is the only state that carries forward. One task per agent keeps each handover small enough to be trustworthy |
| 2026-09-23 | **Agents push `m9-api-v1` after every task**; `main` is never touched and no PR is opened | An unattended multi-day run on one machine is one disk failure away from losing everything. A feature branch is reversible; a merge is not |
| 2026-09-23 | **Q-46 answered: `Scalar.AspNetCore` is approved** (the 7th package). Swagger/Swashbuckle stays ruled out | The owner approved it directly. Serving the reference UI from our own host beats a CDN call for a server that posts money |
| 2026-09-24 | **`Api:Reference:AllowTryIt` ships `true`**: the Scalar page may send live requests | Owner decision, taken knowingly. FR-A-18 ships it **false** so "a reader of the documentation must not be one click away from posting a live transaction", and turning it on is the deliberate act that requirement asks for. Mitigations that remain: `Api:Reference:Enabled` still ships **false**, so the page is not served in Production at all until someone turns it on as well; a request from the page still needs a valid `X-Api-Key`, so the page being open does not by itself let a stranger post; posting routes are capped at 10/min; and startup now logs a Warning naming the route, the way `Api:AllowInsecureRemote` does |
| 2026-09-23 | **The run stops at the end of M9.** Server-verification tasks are left `ready-for-human` and no new milestone is started | T-903's checklist, T-802/803/804 and the direct-post check need a person at the QuickBooks machine. An agent cannot do them and must not claim to |
| 2026-09-15 | Core is cross-platform; COM isolated in QbAutopost.QuickBooks | tests run anywhere; server only for M6/M8 |
| 2026-09-15 | Invoices are evidence only (Q1 assumed) | avoids a second posting pipeline in v1 |
| 2026-09-15 | Deposits post straight to income (Q2 assumed) | pending answer on open customer invoices |
| 2026-09-16 | Build Core from the spec; POC unavailable (ADR-0007) | owner chose to proceed without the POC; gaps listed in Questions |
| 2026-09-16 | ADRs live in `docs/adr/` (0001–0007) | architecture decisions recorded with their alternatives |
| 2026-09-16 | `global.json` pins SDK 8.0.x (`rollForward: latestFeature`) | SDK 10 is installed on the dev box and would otherwise be used |
| 2026-09-16 | JSON enums are written camelCase (`"ready"`, `"post"`, `"ccCharge"`) and read case-insensitively | spec §6/§10 show lowercase values; `rules.json` `"Kind": "Bank"` still loads; `ledger.json` `kind` becomes `"check"` (no real ledger exists yet) |
| 2026-09-16 | M1 already posts through `IQbGateway` (G5 + ledger), with an `UnconfiguredQbGateway` that sends nothing until M6 | T-103 names `RunPostAsync`; the state machine needs a real posting path to test; M6 tasks keep the COM, live-G4, retry, busy and backup work |
| 2026-09-16 | Repo root is `D:\qb_post` (plan calls it `qb-autopost/`); `CLAUDE.md` copied to the root, `docs/CLAUDE.md` kept | plan T-005; copies must be kept in sync until one is removed |
| 2026-09-22 | The "API conversion" is M9, a restructure — not a rewrite. Contract in `docs/spec-api-v1.md`; `spec.md` §6 rewritten at T-914 | the app is already an ASP.NET Core 8 minimal API with 10 routes, API-key auth, problem details and file logging (handoff §3, §6) |
| 2026-09-22 | D-1: keep the job-folder pipeline **and** add a direct JSON-rows post; one engine, two entrances | owner answer; a golden test keeps the qbXML of both paths byte-identical so the engine cannot fork |
| 2026-09-22 | D-2: two validation endpoints — the QuickBooks company file, and the transaction data | owner answer; one overloaded "validate" would mix a COM round trip with an offline check |
| 2026-09-22 | D-3: `/api/v1` prefix, legacy flat routes kept one release; **Scalar** for the reference UI, **no Swagger/Swashbuckle** | owner answer (UI) and owner instruction (2026-09-22, no Swagger). Consequence: .NET 8 has no built-in OpenAPI generator, so the document is hand-authored and drift-tested |
| 2026-09-22 | D-4: remote callers — per-caller keys with scopes, TLS off-loopback, rate limits, 400-day audit | owner answer; the single shared key was safe only because the bind was loopback |
| 2026-10-27 | **M10 starts: PostgreSQL is allowed for saved requests, their history and dashboard users only** (ADR-0009); JSON files stay the store for ledger, rules, lists, job status, idempotency and audit | Owner request, ticket #2. The ledger does not move: G4 and undo read `ledger.json` |
| 2026-10-27 | **Q-80 answered yes: five packages approved** (Npgsql EF provider, EF Design, Identity EF, JwtBearer, Testcontainers.PostgreSql) with the pins recorded in Q-80 | Owner approval, 2026-10-27 |
| 2026-10-27 | Dashboard users sign in with Identity + HMAC JWT (15 min) and a rotating refresh cookie (7 d); `requests:edit/post/admin` and `users:manage` are people-only (ADR-0010) | A named person must press Post; an API key never satisfies those scopes |
| 2026-10-28 | **Owner rulings O-1…O-4 (spec `docs/saved-requests-dashboard-v2.md` §0)**: O-1 no existing API changes; O-2 everything the dashboard needs is a new route under `/api/v2`; O-3 the old routes will be deprecated later (not part of M11); O-4 a "don't post" row is sent as `post: false`, never `kind: "Skip"` | Owner rulings; M11 starts under them |
| 2026-10-28 | **M-1: all of R-10 … R-14 in one milestone (M12)** (spec `docs/dashboard-api-m12.md` §0) | Owner decision, 2026-10-28 |
| 2026-10-28 | **M-2: the upload reads PDFs through OpenRouter directly**, with its own API key; Hermes is not used for this feature | Owner decision, 2026-10-28 |
| 2026-10-28 | **M-3: the extraction prompt is the n8n "Information Extractor" prompt, edited** so every field the model cannot find is left empty; the n8n defaults (`Capital One - 3188`, "Ask My Accountant") are removed; the edited prompt has its own prompt version | Owner decision, 2026-10-28 |
| 2026-10-28 | **M-4: requirements-file rules stand (unchanged):** the PDF is discarded after reading; fields not found stay empty; `controlTotal` is never computed; possible duplicates warn and never block; the user reviews before anything is saved | Owner decision, 2026-10-28 |
| 2026-10-28 | **M-5: the whole app moves from Hermes to OpenRouter**: T1 spec, T2 PDF statements, T3 invoices, T4 judgment and the upload extraction all go through one client and one key; Hermes (127.0.0.1:8642) is used nowhere (spec §14) | Owner decision, 2026-10-28. Statement and invoice text now leaves the server for OpenRouter by the owner's choice |
| 2026-10-28 | **Ruling R1 (M12 plan, T-1202)**: the implementer may mechanically rename config keys `Hermes:X` to `OpenRouter:X` in existing test files (and change a non-loopback `http://` BaseUrl to `https://`), and nothing else in those files; each file is listed in the T-1202 row | The spec removes the Hermes section, so tests that bind it must follow. Cost if wrong: test-file churn the owner sees in the diff |
| 2026-10-28 | **Ruling R2 (M12 plan, T-1212)**: `Should_ListSameFile_When_SavedFileUploadedAgain` seeds the first saved upload through `IUploadStore.SaveUploadAsync` (T-1210) directly, not through the v2 save route, which arrives in T-1213 | The route does not exist until T-1213. Cost if wrong: one test seeds via the store instead of HTTP |

| 2026-09-22 | 14 (cont.) | T-906 | Direct-post request model in Core — the first piece of the JSON path (D-1). RED was CS0234/CS0246 ×4. `DirectRequestReader` turns rows into the **same `StatementLine`s** a statement produces, so the FR-6 mapper, G4, qbXML and G5 are shared rather than reimplemented; `SourceKind`/`Direction` are derived from `kind` and never taken from the caller, so two callers describing one movement fingerprint alike. The refuse-vs-hold split is the heart of it: a control-total mismatch (or missing total, no rows, too many rows, a non-positive or over-precise amount, an unsafe reference) **refuses the whole batch and reads out no lines** — rule 3, no amount is ever adjusted to make a total fit — while an over-cap amount, an out-of-window date or a missing account **holds just that row**, so one bad line cannot fail a batch of hundreds. The control total is checked over every submitted row **including held ones**: it describes what the caller sent, not what survived. `DirectAndFolderPathsAgreeTests` pins the property the two-entrance design rests on — same fingerprint and byte-identical qbXML from either path — and passed on its first run (an invariant over existing code, so no RED; non-vacuity shown by the checkNo test). → **Core 757, Api 292, twice in a row, `-warnaserror` 0 warnings**, commit `6d4505f`. (A) `DirectPlanner` in Core: RED was `CS0246 DirectPlan` → 17 unit tests green. (B) the endpoint: RED was **11 of 12 failing on 404** → 12 green. A third RED came from my own test, not the code: I had faked a ledger fingerprint as `requestId + 48 zeros` while G4 compares the whole SHA-256, so the duplicate test said `OK` instead of `422`; computing it with `Fingerprints.Compute` fixed the test *and* made it an independent check of the spec §7 formula rather than an echo of the API. The design decision worth carrying: the caller's `account` is injected into a **scoped copy of the rules** per (kind, last4) group, so the FR-6 `Mapper` decides exactly as it would for a statement — post-processing its output instead would have lost the payee and tier resolution, because the mapper stops as soon as it holds on `unknown-account`. Two rows sending one last four as two different accounts hold each other (`conflicting-last4`). Status split follows §6.1/§2: a control-total mismatch is **400** (the request contradicts itself), a gate verdict is **422 with the same body as the 200**. `skipped` counts duplicates too, so `wouldPost + held + skipped = submitted` and a caller can check the arithmetic of the answer as well as of the request. Offline is asserted, not assumed: `Gateway.Requests` and `Hermes.Calls` both stay empty. **The qbXML body is withheld even when `?includeQbXml=true` asks for it** — FR-A-6 gates it on a `qb:debug` scope that does not exist until T-910, and handing it to every holder of the shared key is the less careful reading (rule 4); the response says so. | 2026-09-22 | 15 (cont.) | T-908 | `POST /api/v1/quickbooks/transactions` — the direct post, the last of the two pieces that carry money. RED in two cycles: `CS0246` for `ApiBatchStore`/`DirectPostResult`, then **15 of 16 endpoint tests failing on 404**. The design question the handoff flagged — "the batch id feeds the ledger and undo, which today assume a job id per folder" — resolved by **not** inventing a parallel store: `ApiBatchStore` holds evidence only (`request.json`, `status.json`, `request.qbxml`, `response.qbxml`, `result.json` under `Paths:ApiBatches/<batchId>/`), while the `LedgerJob`/`LedgerEntry` rows are written exactly as a folder job writes them. The payoff is that `BatchUndo` and the undo endpoint needed **no change whatsoever** and FR-13 works on a batch that never had a folder — asserted, not assumed. Rule 7 is proven by a test in which QuickBooks throws and `request.json` is on disk regardless: nothing after the send could have written it. A refused request gets no batch id and no folder, so a malformed call leaves nothing undoable-looking behind. §2's 503 is decided by an explicit `Unavailable` field rather than by matching error text, and the body is still the batch so the caller learns its id. The 202 path adds no machinery: `RunExclusiveAsync` already runs work to the end when the caller stops waiting, so the timeout bounds only the wait, and `Prefer: respond-async` is that same path with a zero budget. **Two real defects the tests caught.** The traversal guard used `Path.GetInvalidFileNameChars()`, which on Linux permits `\` and `:` — `C:\windows\system32` would have sailed through and CI would have failed (rule 1); it is now an explicit ASCII allow-list. And adding `Paths:ApiBatches` to the shipped `appsettings.json` made 5 tests write batch evidence to the **real machine folder** `C:\qb-autopost\api-batches` (handoff trap #4 exactly), creating 32 folders before it was noticed; `ApiFactory` now pins the path as it pins every other, the debris was deleted, and a full run afterwards leaves that folder with 0 entries. → **Core 787, Api 320, twice in a row, `-warnaserror` 0 warnings**. New gap Q-59 — FR-A-8's "post a `ready` batch later" route is in no route table and was not built; re-sending with `dryRun:false` re-runs every gate against the rules as they are now, which is the safer of the two. Honest limits in the evidence: no real QuickBooks has seen any of it, evidence is never aged out (Q-52), and two concurrent posts of one reference could still pick the same attempt number — T-909's idempotency store is where that claim belongs. Evidence `docs/testing/T-908.tdd.md` |
| 2026-09-23 | 15 (cont.) | T-909 | `Idempotency-Key` (FR-A-12), in two RED/GREEN cycles: `CS0246` for `IdempotencyStore`, then **7 of 10 host tests failing**. The behaviour that matters is one assertion — the same post sent twice under one key yields one batch, one TxnID, `Idempotency-Replayed: true`, and **exactly one write reaching QuickBooks**. Undo is covered too (two undos, one delete), while **validate deliberately is not**: replaying a validate would hide a change in the rules or the ledger, which is precisely what a validate exists to reveal. Keys are scoped by (key, route, client) so a caller numbering their requests cannot make an undo collide with a post; `clientId` is already part of the key and stays null until T-910, so adding clients later changes no behaviour. Two judgement calls are recorded rather than buried: **a non-2xx releases the key** (Q-60) — holding it would lock a caller out of their own key over a typo, and would block the very retry a 503 invites, which is safe only because idempotency is the convenience layer while G4 and the ledger are the safety layer; and **a replay keeps its original status code** instead of §6.5's blanket 200, since replaying a 202 as 200 would tell a caller their batch had finished when it had not. The middleware sits behind `ApiKeyMiddleware`, so an unauthenticated caller can neither claim a key nor learn from a 409 which keys are in use. Of the four failures after the middleware landed, **three were my tests, not the code**: an empty header cannot be told from an absent one over HTTP, a fixture nested a whole request where a row belonged (the framework rejected it with an empty body before the endpoint ran), and one test read the response body twice, which surfaced as `ObjectDisposedException` rather than a wrong status. The in-progress test also pins a 2 s synchronous budget, so a middleware that stops short-circuiting fails in seconds instead of hanging for two minutes behind a wedged request — which is exactly what the RED run did. → **Core 799, Api 329, twice in a row, `-warnaserror` 0 warnings**, and `C:\qb-autopost` still holds 0 entries after a full run. Evidence `docs/testing/T-909.tdd.md` |
| 2026-09-23 | 15 (cont.) | T-910 | Per-caller keys and scopes (FR-A-13). RED in two cycles: `CS0234` for the new `QbAutopost.Core.Security` namespace, then **8 of 12** scope tests failing. Only a salted hash is stored, asserted by grepping the written file for the plaintext; a read-only client posting gets **403** naming the scope, an unknown key 401, and a revoked client 401 on its **next** request because the list is re-read per request rather than cached to the next restart. Rotation keeps the retiring key working inside its own window. Scopes map **by effect, not by URL**, and an unmapped route falls through to `admin`, so a new endpoint is closed until someone decides otherwise. Gating `?includeQbXml=true` on `qb:debug` **closes the SPEC-GAP T-907 recorded**. Startup now refuses when no enabled client and no usable shared key exist - an empty allow-list answering 401 for ever is a worse failure than one that says so once at boot - and warns whenever the all-scopes legacy key is accepted alongside real clients, since while it is on it outranks every scope decision in `clients.json`. `scripts/new-api-client.ps1` prints the key once and writes only the hash; because it reimplements that hash in PowerShell, a recorded (key, salt, hash) triple from an actual run is asserted against `ApiClientStore.Matches` - without it, a divergence would silently reject every key the script issues, which is the kind of failure discovered at 2am. **A real behaviour change, deliberately taken**: `docs/spec.md` §6 leaves all of `/health/*` open, api-v1 §2.2 opens only `/health` and `/health/ready`, and the narrower rule won on both surfaces because `/health/quickbooks` opens a QuickBooks session and `/health/sdk` probes COM - open to unauthenticated remote callers (D-4), either lets a stranger hammer QuickBooks. That moved **18 existing tests**: some re-pointed at the routes that genuinely need no key, some inverted with the reason written in, some simply given a key, and `LoggingApiTests` updated because a key belonging to no client is now "unknown" rather than "invalid". None was changed merely to silence a failure; §5 of the evidence lists every one. New gap **Q-61** - the owner's monitoring must be told before it starts getting 401. → **Core 820, Api 344, twice in a row, `-warnaserror` 0 warnings**. Evidence `docs/testing/T-910.tdd.md` |
