Nothing had reached QuickBooks at all. `partial` reads as "some of it landed, go and review the holds", so the one word a caller would act on said the opposite of what happened - and `JobStatus.Failed` turned out to be **unreachable on three of the four exits** where nothing had posted, reachable only from the backup guard. The suite had a test for one row held out of two (`partial`, correctly) and none at all for *every* row held, which is the shape of gap a passing suite cannot see: **the tests covered the interesting case and skipped the degenerate one**. Fixed by deriving the status **once**, in `Finish`, from the counts it already computes, and deleting the five branch-level `Status =` assignments - four exits each deciding for themselves is how they came to disagree, and patching the two the owner hit would have left the other two lying. The deliberate departure from the spec's literal "failed (nothing posted)" is recorded rather than smuggled: a **skipped** row does not fail a batch, because G4 skips what QuickBooks already holds, so an all-duplicate re-send posted nothing *because nothing needed posting* - that is the gate working, and calling it `failed` would teach an operator to ignore the word. Only a **held** row or an `error` makes an empty batch `failed`. `Should_ReportPosted_When_EveryRowWasAlreadyInQuickBooks` pins that carve-out and **passed the moment it was written**, so it is named as a regression guard, not offered as evidence. **One existing assertion was changed and is named, not buried**: `Should_WriteTheRequestBeforeAnythingIsSent_When_QuickBooksFails` asserted `partial` - it had encoded the defect; its ordering and empty-ledger assertions, which are what that test exists for, are untouched. Changing a test to fit a code change is forbidden; changing one to fit **the spec the code was violating** is the opposite, and a dedicated test now covers the case explicitly rather than incidentally. Beside it, an **owner decision** applied to configuration: `QuickBooks:AllowedDateWindow:MaxAgeDays` **730 -> 3000** so the 2019 statement falls inside the window. The C# default in `DirectLimits` stays **730**, so only the shipped file leans and the next installation copying the class default still gets the conservative answer. The cost is stated plainly in the evidence: that gate was the only thing standing between a caller and a posting into a closed accounting period, and on this server it no longer is. -> **Core 866 (unchanged), Api 510 (was 507), twice in a row and a third time after the config change, `-warnaserror` 0 warnings**. Evidence `docs/testing/T-919.tdd.md`, §6 of which is plain about what is untouched: `200 OK` is still the answer for a batch that posted nothing, so a client reading only the HTTP code learns nothing from this; **the folder path (`JobPipeline`) derives its own status and was not examined**; and no live QuickBooks was involved in either the report or the fix |
| 2026-09-24 | 29 | T-920 | **The bug that had already wasted an hour of this session, twice.** Running the host by hand during T-919 turned up `DirectoryNotFoundException: ...\bin\samples\rules.json` on every route that loads rules - and later the same resolution silently **misled** me: probing whether the unknown-name check fires, I edited `src/QbAutopost.Api/data/qb-lists.json` while the host was reading `bin/Debug/net8.0/data/qb-lists.json`, and reported that the check does not fire. It does. Both were one cause: `appsettings.Development.json` carried `../../samples/rules.json`, a path written for the project folder, while the content root is `AppContext.BaseDirectory` - **deliberately**, since session-13's published exe started from `C:\Users\...` found no `appsettings.json` at all and ran with Hermes enabled against the wrong configuration. So the content root was never the thing to change. Two defects behind one symptom, and the one worth fixing was **not** the path: `Rules.Load` let a raw IO exception escape into a bare `500` with no problem details, violating the RFC 7807 convention, in a system that **already knew and already said so** - `StartupLog` warns "every job will fail until it exists" and `/health/ready` reports `rules: false`. It simply threw when asked to work. Running without the file is an allowed state by design, so the fix is `503` with a body, not a refusal to start. Four decisions worth their lines: checked **in `Rules.Load`**, because five call sites read that file and fixing the endpoint that happened to be noticed would have left `JobPipeline`, `QbListSync` and the folder entrance throwing - the folder-path test exists to pin exactly that, and it covers a route nobody had reported; **typed, not `catch (IOException)`**, because a missing parent folder and a missing file raise different exceptions and neither is distinguishable from a failing disk, so the handler matches one type and returns `false` for all else, leaving a real fault looking like a fault; the detail **names the resolved path**, with its own test, because the entire defect was a path resolving somewhere nobody expected while every message said only that something was missing; and Development is fixed by **copying the file beside the executable in Debug only** rather than counting `../` - the correct depth differs between `bin/Debug/net8.0`, Release and publish, so a literal path would be right in exactly one configuration and quietly wrong in the rest, while `CopyToPublishDirectory="Never"` keeps rules naming fictional accounts off a machine that posts real money. **No test existed for this at all**: `Readiness` was covered and `StartupLog` was covered, but *what a request does in that state* was not - which is how a route answered `500` inside a suite of 510 green tests. -> **Core 866 (unchanged), Api 513 (was 510), twice in a row, `-warnaserror` 0 warnings**, and verified against the **running host with no environment override**: `200` where it had been `500`, `/health/ready` naming the resolved path. Evidence `docs/testing/T-920.tdd.md`, §5 of which records what is deliberately **not** fixed: a **corrupt or empty** rules file still throws into a bare `500`, because widening the handler to `JsonException` would catch a caller's own malformed body and answer `503` to a client error - **Q-73**, not guessed at. Also left alone: `Paths:QbLists` shares the resolution but does not throw - `QbListsStore.Load` returns `Empty`, which **silently disables the unknown-name check**, and whether that silence is right is its own question |
| 2026-09-24 | 30 | T-921…T-924 | **A new route that posts money, and the silent hole it very nearly shipped through.** `POST /api/v1/quickbooks/transactions/validate-and-post` (ADR-0008): validate the whole payload, and either report every finding and send nothing, or post all of it and return the `TxnID`s — one request, one response, no `dryRun`, no `202`, no batch to poll. The hole: `ApiRoutes.ScopeFor` matched the posting route by **exact string**, so the new path fell through to `StartsWith("/quickbooks/")` and resolved to **`qb:read`** — the scope issued for reading lists. A route that moves real money would have been gated behind the read scope, with the route working and every test green. Everywhere else an unmapped route falls to `admin`, which is closed; under `/quickbooks/` the fall-through opens instead. Now an explicit branch, pinned by a 403 through the real host and by a route-level test. Almost nothing else is new code: `DirectPlanner` decides the rows and `DirectPostRunner` performs the post, both unchanged — **`DirectPostApiTests` was not edited and is green, so FR-A-8 is provably untouched**. The genuinely new parts are `JobQueue.TryRunExclusiveAsync` (the worker lock claimed with one `Interlocked.CompareExchange`, so of many simultaneous callers exactly one is admitted and the rest get `409` without being enqueued) and one early return: any held row ends the request at `422` before a single byte reaches QuickBooks, proved by `Assert.Empty(Gateway.Requests)` — not even G4's read-only queries. Core 866 → 867, Api 513 → 549, three consecutive green runs, 0 warnings. A real race in the new lock surfaced on the third run — the claim was released after the worker moved on, but the waiting caller resumes from inside the work, so two sequential posts could see a spurious 409; released before the result is published now, and pinned. Q-A…Q-D recorded as **Q-74…Q-77** with the conservative choice taken for each; new gap **Q-78** (§6.1 promises `status: posted` on a 200, but G4's live half and G5 run after the SDK is touched, so a post-SDK rejection has no ending in §5 — answered with the true status rather than a reassuring one). **ADR-0008 stays `proposed`**: the plan says to accept it once Q-A and Q-D are answered, and nobody has answered them. T-923/T-924 are `ready-for-human` — the checklist is in `docs/testing/T-921-T-924.tdd.md` §9, and it needs `samples/validate-and-post-request.json`, not `samples/request.json`, because that one carries `"dryRun": true` which this route refuses by design |
